ParlaMint-PL_2022-01-12-sejm-46-1.u17	Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! I have the pleasure of submitting a draft law amending the Act on taxation on goods and services from print No. 1905 submitted by a group of Members of the Law and Justice. Ladies and gentlemen! Ladies and gentlemen, once again, I would like to ask those Members who are not taking part in this point to leave the room and to allow the Member to present the bill. Sir, just a moment, sir. Okay? Hold on a second, please. The draft law provides for amendments to the Act of 11 March 2004 on VAT on goods and services, hereinafter referred to as the VAT Act, the main purpose of which is to implement measures in the field of taxation aimed at countering the effects of inflation. Inflation is today a phenomenon, ladies and gentlemen, a global phenomenon, unfortunately also affecting our country very strongly. The causes of global inflation, but also European inflation, are being said a lot. The main causes of the inflationary process are the world's largest price shock in the 1990s, the rise in energy prices caused, among others. European Union climate policy, a significant increase in food prices, the largest in the 1930s, metal ore and other raw materials for the production of industrial chemistry, wood, not to mention gas, which in our part Europe has been costing more than 600% in the last year. Ladies and gentlemen, the project provides for a temporary reduction of VAT rates between 1 February 2022 and 31 July 2022, i.e. for a period of 6 months, for food covered so far at a rate of 5% to the rate of 0. Ladies and gentlemen, we will have a zero food rate in our country for the next six months. For motor fuels: diesel, self-fuel biocomponents, motor gasoline, LPG, at a rate of 23% We're down to 8%. The reduction is to be at 15% . So far, this is the biggest reduction in fuel in our country's history. Fertilizers, plant protection products, gardening grounds and other... [[Game in the O.R.]] [[Piotr Zgorzelski, Vice-Marshal of the Sejm, is the chairman of the proceedings.]] Mr President, could you stop for a moment, please? All Members who are discussing in the Parliament's Chamber, please move this discussion out of the Chamber so as to allow, firstly, the speech of the Member and, secondly, those who are interested, a meaningful participation in the debate, because it is not heard at the moment. Thank you very much, Marshal. For fertilisers, plant protection products, gardening land and other agricultural products, such as soil improvers, growth promoters and some crop substrates, we propose to change the rate from 8% to 0. Thus, both in the agricultural production area, i.e. all components related to fertilisers, plant products and food will be at 0 for the next 6 months. The 0 rate is also proposed for natural gas. We're going to lower this rate, I hope, after we vote this law to the 0 rate. For electricity and heat, a rate of 5% is proposed. The amendments proposed in the Act are, ladies and gentlemen, a continuation of the anti-inflationary measures in the first inflationary shield developed in 2021, thus continuing the tax reductions introduced from 1 January 2022. The purpose of the temporary reductions in VAT rates on the goods in question is to stop the price increase on goods and therefore also to help combat the effects of inflation for all households in Poland, for all taxpayers, bearing in mind that really global processes affect prices in our country today. It can be assumed that reducing these VAT rates should in principle improve the quality of life of families, households in the coming months. The first anti-inflationary shield, ladies and gentlemen, has brought inflation levels up to 1 to 1.5%, as experts say. We hope that in the case of an anti-inflationary shield 2.0, which we are talking about at this point, is going to be similar. The reduction in VAT rates between 1 February and 31 July 2022, i.e. for 6 months, will result in a reduction in the revenue of the State's VAT budget by approximately PLN 11 600 million, and here I present an estimate of PLN 11 600 million. We must be aware that on the one hand this PLN 11 600 million will be part of support for all the people of our country, but let us also remember that real tax revenues will fall. Ladies and gentlemen, food and beverages currently covered by the 5% rate are proposed to cover a 0-percent rate and the losses will amount to PLN 2 920 million. This is the anticipation of food and drink. For motor fuels, i.e. diesel, biocomponents that are essential fuel, motor gasoline and liquefied gas, the rate will fall from 23 to 8%. As I said, VAT will be reduced by 15 percentage points during this period. This is a loss for the state budget at the level of PLN 3100 million. In the field of fertilisers and plant protection products, gardening land and other agricultural production aids, the growth promoters mentioned here and some substrates for cultivation, the rate will be reduced from 8 to 0%. The value of this aid will be around PLN 520 million as estimated in 2020. Support for our citizens with regard to natural gas resulting from the reduction of VAT rates to 0% it will oscillate around 2 billion - it will amount to slightly more than 2 billion PLN. In terms of electricity, there will be a decrease of up to 5%, which means the amount of PLN 2300 million. In the case of heat, the reduction in the rate will result in about PLN 670 million. And let me repeat: helping our citizens with the anti-inflationary shield 2.0 will result in a sum, ladies and gentlemen, of more than PLN 11 billion. Over PLN 11 billion our citizens will pay less in case of growth, unfortunately, the level of inflation. We hope, therefore, that this will slow down this process, which, as I said earlier, is not only dependent on internal factors, but, as indicated by the National Bank of Poland, and not only, also the International Monetary Fund and the World Bank, is primarily the merit of global factors, including those I have mentioned. The most important factor for Polish inflation is the rise in prices of energy carriers, from CO2 prices to electricity prices to gas prices. What's important, ladies and gentlemen. In the proposed draft law in Art. 2 We find a provision which refers to the obligation to place at the register office the trader selling food, motor fuels or goods used for agricultural production clear information on the reduction of VAT rates corresponding to the goods which the seller is trading. This is, ladies and gentlemen, information that is to be a sure indication for citizens. It also indicates that we support the very active fight against inflation. We are not a political group that has assumed that inflation or the possibility of fighting it is something completely independent of us, so another anti-inflationary shield, called the anti-inflationary shield 2.0, has been proposed for over PLN 11 billion. We are convinced that flattens are this process and to some extent will also ease the accounts of all citizens who will be paid in our country in the coming months. It must be hoped that literally in the moment when we are dealing with the first spring months, these prices on the world markets will also fall in terms of electricity, but above all in terms of gas. This will also be an element that will cause the process to flatten and reduce inflation. What is important, ladies and gentlemen - in the proposed bill in article. 2 We find a provision which refers to the obligation to place at the register office the trader selling food, motor fuels or goods used for agricultural production clear information on the reduction of VAT rates corresponding to the goods which the seller is trading. I would like to ask Mrs Teresa Wargock to submit a report to the committee. [[Voice from the room: Now, now, now, now, here comes a friend.]] But no one is rushing the Member, we all see that he is trying to get to the Sejm stand as soon as possible.
ParlaMint-PL_2022-01-12-sejm-46-1.u101	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! I present a report by the Committee on Education, Science and Youth and the Committee on National Defence on the Government bill amending the Law - Educational Law and some other laws, print No 1812. Marshal of the Sejm, according to Art. Article 37 (1) (a) and (b) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 1 and Article 1 Article 40 (1) (a) and (b) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 On 30 November 2021, 1 of the Rules of Procedure of the Sejm addressed the above draft law to the Committee on Education, Science and Youth and the Committee on National Defence. The joint committee meeting on which the first reading and examination of the project took place took place on 4 January. During the committee's work, after overcoming technical difficulties in handling remote work, Minister Dariusz Piontkowski presented the bill. Development of the draft statutory changes is a further improvement of the legal basis for the functioning of the education system in Poland. Regulations were introduced concerning the establishment and supervision of military preparation units - at the request of the Minister of National Education and the possibility of setting up branch schools subject to artistic schools - at the request of the Minister of Culture and National Heritage. In addition, the Act introduces an obligation to organise distance learning, remote teaching in case of suspension of classes for pupils for reasons other than COVID, if the suspension of these classes lasts more than 2 days, and the possibility of organising remote teaching for students covered by individual teachings or hospitalized pupils. The Act sets out the rules for non-governmental organisations in schools and institutions. In addition, a catalogue of the conditions to be met has been introduced in the Act in order for the host body to be able to liquidate the school or establishment. The Act strengthens the position of education curator in the area of common competences of the leading authority and the pedagogical supervisory authority. I will say very generally: the previous activities of the lead authority which required the opinion of the curator, in principle in these areas require a positive opinion of the curator in accordance with the wording of the draft. The position of the pedagogical board has been strengthened, an obligation has been introduced to evaluate the organisational sheet by the pedagogical board. The catalogue of data in the educational information system has been extended to include school tasks for pupils with a ruling on the need for special education and re-education classes and early development support opinions. It also regulates the transfer of data on the employment of teachers to the Social Insurance Institution. The draft law also includes new rules on the functioning of non-governmental organisations in schools and institutions. The debate at the committee meeting, the debate on this bill was very long and very hot, I can say colloquially. In principle, the opposition Members questioned mainly Article. 86 introducing the provision that the school curator is giving an opinion on the proposal made by the school principal and the non-governmental organisation in the area of the activities to be conducted with the students, the methods used in conducting the classes and the staff to conduct the classes in the school. The long debate of the opposition also concerned the issue of strengthening the education curator. I will not go into the details of the debate because it lasted a few hours, but I can say that the amendments tabled by the Members of the opposition and the votes in the debate show that the changes do not gain acceptance and that the opposition would like these changes not to be introduced because they are kind of exceeding the legal status which the Education Act introduces as regards the division of competences between the host authority and the pedagogical supervisory authority. The Minister, referring to this discussion, made it very clear that the remit of the lead authority in terms of the tasks it performs in the education system does not change. Conducting bodies continue to pursue educational policies to ensure that education is accessible to all pupils and young people. They have tasks in the area of good technical condition of buildings. They have tasks in the area of being an employer for the school principal. And these tasks are defined by law. The pedagogical supervision, as you know, is carried out by the state. The state has an educational policy. In the area of pedagogical supervision, there are issues of compliance with the provisions of the Education Law, the functioning of schools. These are the basic documents of the programme basis, regulations in the area of evaluation, classification, promotion and admission of pupils to schools. The legal status is uniform throughout Poland and there is supervision of the curator. But what is most important is the implementation of the curriculum basis and the objectives of the school at the different educational stages that are written in the programme basis. There is also pedagogical supervision of compliance with the school statute. The Minister stressed very clearly, and I personally fully agree, that such opinions, that the school is losing its autonomy, should not take place, because the school is not, as is higher education, legally recorded autonomy. In the area of education, we are talking about the autonomy of the teacher and this autonomy of the teacher in choosing teaching methods and achieving educational goals is absolutely intact. On the contrary, many activities have been undertaken in the Ministry of Education over the past years, which provide additional tools and teaching aids. Absolutely no teacher is restricted in his autonomy. After the discussion, a motion was made to reject the draft law. Mrs Dziemianowicz-Bąk's motion was rejected by 36 votes to 38. Ms Krystyna Szumilas has requested a public hearing. The motion was rejected by 37 votes in favour, 39 against. Mr Piotr Borys has requested that the bill be sent to the subcommittee. This motion was rejected by 35 votes in favour, 40 against. We've moved on to the bill. I'm just going to make a very big reference, so to speak, exactly. This amendment was tabled by Mrs Krystyna Shumilas. I think that this amendment corresponds to the debate that has been held. She has tabled an amendment on amending the title of the law and has proposed that the law should have the title: on the manual control of the school by politicians and on the right to raise children to parents. [[Applause]] Of course, this is a very good amendment, very serious, very substantive, very consistent with the legislative process. Ladies and gentlemen, let's really, really, really make the law, not like this, you can say, rhetorical fun. In the course of the drafting of the bill, the clubs tabled amendments. The Civic Coalition has tabled 22 amendments, Law and Justice - 11 amendments, Left - 6 amendments and Confederation - 1 amendment. In the process of examining and voting on individual amendments... Oh, one more piece of paper, that'll be it. The final result of the committee's work is the adoption of 11 amendments to Law and Justice. I will briefly describe what changes the committee has made to the draft print bill 1812. As a result of the amendments adopted, the procedure for appealing the school head or establishment under Article was harmonised. 55 and Article 55 of Regulation (EU) No 182/2011 of the European Parliament and of the Council of 16 February 2011 laying down the rules and general principles concerning mechanisms for control by Member A transitional provision was also introduced for applications for cancellation of the school head submitted before the entry into force of the law - in general the law currently in force, the applications will be dealt with according to the current legal status. Amendment, with the approval of the Ministry, after discussion in the parliamentary club, concerning the withdrawal from changing the composition of the competition committee in the competition for the position of head of the school. We have returned to the provisions currently in force, the compositions of the competition committees do not change, as well as the powers of trade unions in the delegation of the members of the competition committee. A very important amendment was adopted introducing deadlines for the publication by the bodies holding the term of the competition for the post of school head. This was also due to the conclusions of pedagogical supervision. Unfortunately, there have been, although very isolated, situations in which directors, with whom the lead authority wanted to part, to obtain an evaluation of the work, were generally prevented, these competitions were announced at a time convenient for the lead authority. So we are introducing a provision aimed at rigidly determining at what time this competition can take place, so that those participating in the competition can complete the documentation, attach the certificates and get an evaluation of the work. In this context, we have also amended the provision that, in the case of a school principal, an evaluation of the work is given by the pedagogical supervisor in agreement with the local authority, with the lead authority. Unfortunately, there were cases where this assessment could not be agreed. In a situation where there is no such arrangement, we have accepted the solution that the education curator is presenting the evaluation. We have also introduced in the form of an amendment a solution aimed at obliging the school head to inform the pedagogical supervisory authority about changes in the school's organisational sheet introduced after 30 September. Here, too, there were very serious signals from teachers concerning unfortunately such activities of the school management, that the teacher generally learned in August before the pedagogical council starting the next school year, what is the allocation of hours, what are the jobs, what are the solutions. Thus, in this procedure of opinion of the organisational sheet, which is provided for in the Act, is also to familiarize the pedagogical board before sending for opinion by the curator. There are three amendments to the amendments, the amendments can be said to be technical. These are changes in the date of entry into force of the proposed law. These amendments are really technical, it concerns the creation of artistic schools, the deadline for submitting an application for permission to establish a branch of military preparation and the entry into force of the entire draft law, because we are already a little later with the legislative process in the Sejm. Perhaps one more sentence important for the report. Members have tabled 11 amendments as proposals by a minority who have not received a positive opinion from the committee. ParlaMint-PL_2022-01-12-sejm-46-1.u103	Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! On behalf of the Parliamentary Club, Law and Justice, I present my position on the government draft law amending the Law - Educational Law and some other laws, print No 1812. The planned amendment to the amendment to the Act - Educational Law, among others. strengthens the role of the pedagogical supervisor, which is a consistent implementation of the Law and Justice electoral programme. In order to carry out statutory tasks, the curator should be provided with appropriate legal provisions excluding any application of the Educational Law. At this point I would like to reassure all school heads and institutions, because the demonization of this law by the majority of the opposition has nothing to do with the solutions proposed in the legislation. There is no legal regulation in the Education Law regarding the completion of the recommendation procedure and the effective supervision of non-public institutions and schools. Very few, but there were situations where the school principal did not make the recommendations made or the head of a non-public school, and very often he is also the lead authority, prevented the audit from being carried out. The proposed changes regulate and equip the school curator with appropriate tools to effectively carry out surveillance tasks. The role of supervision has been strengthened in the case of the appointment of a director for less than 5 years, the appointment of a director if he is not selected as a result of a competition or the appointment of a non-teacher as a school principal. The draft law introduces an obligation for the authority to obtain a positive opinion from the school curator in these cases. In the same project as the critics of the law, the legal protection of school heads was strengthened, making the teacher's dismissal from the management position during the school year without giving notice to receive a positive opinion from the school curator. Currently, the guardian's opinion was not binding for the lead authority. For the benefit of school heads, provision has been made for the lack of agreement between the authority on the assessment of their work. Until now, the lack of agreement between the lead authority and the pedagogical supervisor has prevented the evaluation of the Director, which has often prevented the Director from applying for another term of office. Now, in the event of a disagreement, the education curator will issue an evaluation. The conditions of activity in schools of associations and organisations have been regulated, which so far depended on a positive opinion of the pedagogical board and the parents' advice. The proposal for amendments also made the conduct of these classes subject to the consent of the school curator, who, in the course of pedagogical supervision, ensures the implementation of the curriculum in schools and institutions, but, above all, for the first time, the requirement to obtain written consent from parents, who must be informed by the school principal of the objectives and content of the programme. I can therefore assure my parents that when the law enters into force, no out-of-school organization, except for scout organisations, will be able to conduct classes with their children without their consent. The bill also contains a number of other amendments. The term of the competition for the post of head of the school has been introduced, which is a good solution for the current headmaster in the event of an intention to run for office in due course to ask for an evaluation of the work. It also prevents a competition from being held one year or more in advance. The Act, which is a novel, sets out the conditions that must be fulfilled in order for the school or establishment to be wound up. In addition, the proposed law clarifies that a local government unit may indicate that pupils can only continue to study in schools or schools run by that unit. The draft law also introduced proposals for changes concerning military preparation units in secondary schools developed in cooperation with the Ministry of National Defence. A new regulation has been introduced on the obligation to organise classes for pupils using distance learning methods and techniques where there is no traditional possibility of conducting classes. Regulations have also been proposed to allow the municipality to organise the transport of pupils in situations other than those in which the municipality has a statutory obligation. The deadline for adapting the housing conditions of pre-school units in primary schools was extended until 31 August 2024. Parliamentary Club Law and Justice, taking the above into account, will vote in favour of this law. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

We've been taking advantage of the experience of the last few months. This is about the possibility of introducing remote learning in situations where the school cannot function, for example. There's a storm, a storm that partially destroyed the building, and you can't go on with your fixed education there. Similarly, I answer the question of one of my fellow Members here - there is the possibility of remote learning in the case of individual teaching or children who are in medical establishments. ParlaMint-PL_2022-01-12-sejm-46-1.u291	Thank you very much. Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! On behalf of the Committee on Energy, Climate and State Assets and the Committee on the Environment, Natural Resources and Forestry, I present a report on the draft Parliamentary Law amending the Act - Water Law and some other laws. The draft is contained in print No 1843, and the committee's report is in print No 1851. This draft law amends the Act of 20 July 2017 - Water Law, the Act of 19 July 2019 amending the Act on the provision of environmental information and its protection, public participation in environmental protection and on environmental impact assessments and certain other laws, as well as the Act of 13 February 2020 on the amendment of the Act - Construction Law and certain other laws. High Izbo! The aim of the project is to decide which provisions are to be applied in the proceedings concerning the environmental conditions which were issued before the decision to establish the location of the investment in the construction of a nuclear power plant, issued on the basis of a speciality of 29 June 2011, in respect of which the investor has already requested a decision on the environmental conditions. The aim is to ensure that the environmental procedures to which this investment is subject are carried out in accordance with the law currently in force for all other investments. The procedure for issuing decisions on environmental conditions for the construction and operation of the first Polish nuclear power plant was initiated at the request made in 2015. The environmental impact assessment for such a project - which can always have a significant impact on the environment - requires the verification of the environmental impact report, and therefore in 2015 the investor requested that the scope of such a report be determined. The competent authority has issued a provision specifying the scope of the report, as well as a provision to suspend proceedings on the issue of environmental considerations until the investor has submitted a report on environmental impact. The report after these few years is ready, the proceedings may be suspended, the competent authority may proceed with the adoption of decisions on environmental conditions, but since 2015, the provisions mentioned by me at the beginning of the laws have changed in a very important way. Failure to take these changes into account in the proceedings will result in its failure, inefficiency and delays, and that is not what we want. Administration should be efficient, effective. I would like to remind you that the Act - Water Law adopted on 20 July 2017, a very extensive law, probably the most extensive in the last term of the Sejm, the Act for which I was rapporteur, implemented eight directives, amended 45 laws in force, including - Article. 509 - to the Environmental Impact Assessment Act. There have also been amendments to the Law - Construction Law and, as I say, many other laws. As far as the transitional and final provisions are concerned, there were 50 articles in this law finally adopted on 20 July 2017 and 19 laws amending the Water Law have been adopted since 20 July 2017. High Izbo! All the laws, the regulations of which standardize the localisation procedure, have been amended several times since 2015. In the Law referred to in Article. 2 of this print project No 1843, i.e. the Act of 19 July 2019 amending the Act on the provision of environmental information and its protection, public participation in environmental protection and environmental impact assessments - that was print No 3616 in the last term - has been fully transposed by the Evaluation Directive, i.e. Directive 2011/92/EU. It is not, therefore, as the opposition Members held in the debate that we had in the first reading of the joint committee, that the draft print law 1843 narrows down the public's participation in procedures, that the fact that we will apply the current wording narrows down the public's participation in procedures, that the inhabitants of the places where the investment will be located will not have access to the localisation procedure, and it is not true that environmental organisations will have difficult access to the conduct. Ladies and gentlemen! High Izbo! Ladies and gentlemen, this Parliamentary print project 1843 does not address this issue at all. On the basis of my experience with working in the environmental committee, I will say that it is quite the opposite, because one of the environmental laws examined in the environmental committee a few months ago just increases the power to participate in court proceedings, environmental proceedings for environmental organisations. So that all that the opposition is saying here is unfortunately just fairy tales, I do not hesitate to use that word. That's another line. Act of 19 July 2019, which I refer to, amended in Article. 2 amended the technical regulations. Ladies and gentlemen, this 2019 amendment has led to a change in the decision-making bodies. The rules for establishing the parties have also changed, and in this respect the determining parameter has become the distance from the planned investment and not, as previously in the legislation, the numbers from the land register of neighbouring parcels. It was a very bad solution, because sometimes the party was someone whose site was 5 km away from the planned investment, because that was the configuration of the neighboring plot, and it had to be notified, and the person living or living even close to the investment, but who was separated from it by the road, was not a party. So these were just very unreasonable regulations, which have been changed and rightly, because they better protect the rights of neighbours of various kinds of investments. High Izbo! In turn, the law which is amended in Article. 3 projects, i.e. The Law of 13 February 2020 introduced the digitisation of procedures in the field of construction law and the Law on spatial planning and development. In the course of detailed work on the draft, legislators raised issues of a kind of bad legislative practice as regards the creation of provisions with retroactive effect, in particular as regards transitional provisions, where, in the case of the Law - Water Law as amended by Article. 1 project dates back 4 years. Similar reservations, i.e. distortions of legal certainty and contradictions with the principle of trust in the law created have been reported by legislators in relation to the article. 2 and 3 of the project, except that the 2019 and 2020 laws are being amended there, i.e. this period is slightly shorter. However, it must be noted with all responsibility that we are dealing with a change in the transitional provisions to a very narrow extent, as this concerns only one proceedings suspended since 2016. It is the procedure referred to in Article. 72. 1 point 18a of the Evaluation Act, because all articles refer to this provision. So there will be no legal chaos, as the Members of the opposition say. Legal chaos could be expected in the absence of the amendments discussed today, because of the three laws that we are amending, i.e. the Water Law, the law amending the 2019 Assessment Act and the law amending the construction law of 2020, part of it says that the new rules apply, and part of it says that the old rules apply. Thus, the conduct of proceedings in which each law, which is simply an integral part of the process of granting permission to enter the territory, says otherwise, would simply lead to legal chaos. The purpose of the amendment is to decide which legal regime is to apply in the case of proceedings concerning a decision on environmental conditions issued before obtaining a decision to establish the location of investments in the construction of a nuclear plant issued under the Law of 29 June 2011 on the preparation and implementation of investments in nuclear installations and associated investments for which the investor has already requested a decision on environmental conditions. So we're talking about one single situation, but it's extremely important. It is one of those that can always have a significant impact on the environment and therefore the environmental impact assessment in its case includes in particular the verification of the environmental impact report. The investor requested a provision setting out the scope of the report in 2015. In view of the above, the authority competent for the decision on environmental conditions has issued a provision specifying the scope of the report and a provision to suspend proceedings on environmental considerations until the investor has submitted a report on the environmental impact of the project. This is simply the order of these actions. Since 2015, the procedure for issuing the above mentioned document the decision remains suspended. During that period, the provisions on the basis of which the competent authorities have acted on the issue of environmental considerations have changed repeatedly and significantly. The transitional provisions accompanying these changes did not always take into account the specificities of the suspended procedure on decisions on environmental conditions for nuclear energy. Therefore, it was considered appropriate to introduce provisions updating the legal status to be taken into account when making decisions on the environmental conditions for this project. The amendments provided for in the proposed wording are intended to improve the process of issuing environmental decisions, including ensuring the participation of specialised bodies in the process of issuing that decision. It should be stressed that the proposed solution increases legal certainty on the part of administrative authorities and the parties to the proceedings for decisions. It is therefore necessary to amend the proposed amendments without delay. Parliamentary Club Law and Justice accepts the legal solutions proposed in the joint report of the Commission: Energy, Climate and State Assets and Environmental Protection, Natural Resources and Forestry, print 1851, as they will significantly speed up the formal preparation of the construction of a nuclear power plant in Poland. What do we read in the explanatory memorandum to this law? That it was the only procedure to decide on the environmental conditions for such an investment and it was initiated by the investor in 2015. On the basis of what bill? The 2011 Act. Ladies and gentlemen, have we forgotten that we have a strategic investment in nuclear energy, making legislative changes, which were mostly government projects, we have forgotten that it is important to ensure that transitional provisions respect this fact? Apparently so. The authority competent to issue this decision suspended the proceedings, expecting the investor, i.e. Polskie Elektrowni Elektrowni Elektrowni Łądowych, to present the relevant report. This report is now being finalised, in the meantime in 2016 some substantive scope of this report and spatial scope is to be introduced. The so-called. the scoping procedure which has defined these requirements and consequently the work is being finalised at the moment. It has turned out that the absence of transitional provisions in the three laws concerned does not facilitate this procedure, and therefore it is necessary to add transitional provisions to these three laws at the moment, including amendments to the Act - Water law, which, as the rapporteur said, affects amendments to 45 laws and is linked to the implementation of eight directives. However, in the meantime, it was also the Water Law of 2017 that was amended: the Act of 19 July 2019 amending the Act on the making available of environmental information and its protection, public participation in environmental protection and on environmental impact assessments and certain other laws, as well as the Act of 13 February 2020 amending the Act - Construction Law, which concerns the electronicisation of proceedings. Therefore, we have the 2011 Investment Implementation Act - on this basis the proceeding has started. In 2016, we have a scoping procedure on the content of the report, there are four further three changes, major changes: in 2017, 2019 and 2020 in the said laws and we do not take into account that the ongoing proceedings need to be taken into account. It is only in 2021 that the government discovers that the procedure cannot be completed efficiently without changing the provisions of the three laws mentioned here, because they do not contain transitional provisions for the most important strategic investment, which is the nuclear power plant. I have no doubt that this is a strategic investment and we are making changes, introducing transitional provisions after 4 years, and it is a novel on the initiative of the Members. What does the Legislative Bureau say? It says that it is a bad practice when we introduce provisions with retroactive effect, and it is still within the scope of transitional provisions, and the clock is withdrawn here by 4 years in the case of at least one of the laws, and we also do not have Polish energy policy, but we only have it established by 2040. It would be good for us to work on the basis of the current energy strategy of Poland. So we have, High Sejm, not only the problem with the Polish Ład, but we also have a problem with the legislative order, because this example shows that we do not use all the possibilities that we have in the state institutions. After all, there is a Legislative Council and there are procedures for dealing with government projects, and it seems to me that this is the right stage for us to bear in mind, especially if this concerns strategic investments. Because if there were more than one of these proceedings initiated under the 2011 Act, we could say, "Well, it's gone through there somewhere." [[♪ The bell ♪]] But there's one strategic investment, and right now we have to fix it after a few years. It doesn't add up to this project that I'm going to say there, the value of a project that you'd like to vote on. Thank you very much.

ParlaMint-PL_2022-01-12-sejm-46-1.u361	Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! I present a report by the Committee on Social Policy and Family Affairs on the draft law amending the Act on Social Employment and amending the Act on Sobriety Education and Against Alcoholism from Print No. 1834. Your Marshal of the Sejm, based on Art. Article 37 (1) (a) and (b) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 1 and Article 1 Article 40 (1) (a) and (b) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 1 sent the above proposal to work in the Committee on Social Policy and Family Affairs. The Committee on Social Policy and Family Policy carried out its first reading and examined this draft law on 11 January this year. High Izbo! The Social Employment Act of 13 June 2003 counters the marginalisation of social groups and the consequences of social exclusion through labour and social reintegration implemented by social integration centres and social inclusion clubs. In view of changing socio-economic conditions, in particular the labour market situation, it has become necessary to amend the provisions of the Social Employment Act. These changes, included in the draft law, will improve the situation of participants in social inclusion centres and clubs, facilitate the functioning of these institutions and improve the quality of functioning of social employment entities. Thus, the draft law contains the following proposals. Proposals that improve the situation of participants in clubs and social inclusion centres are: increasing the level of integration benefit to 120% unemployment benefit for the entire duration of the activities of the Social Inclusion Centre, including the probationary period; more flexible participation in the Centre up to 30 hours per week; longer participation in the activities of the Integration Centre by 6 months, up to a total of 24 months; additional free days, in total 12, including the maintenance of the integration benefit; up to 100% of the maximum incentive premium paid by the Social Inclusion Centre's own resources integration benefits. There are also proposals for changes that facilitate the functioning of social inclusion centres and clubs. These proposals include: abandoning the conditions of poverty when running social assistance centres to participate in classes; allowing social cooperatives to set up centres and clubs for both legal persons and individuals; enabling social and professional reintegration services to be carried out within a functioning social services centre in a different location from the centre; enabling the Marshal to receive grants for the equipment of the centre in the event of its expansion or the creation of workshops in another municipality - now such a grant is only for the equipment - and extending the catalogue of sources of funding for the activities of social inclusion clubs to grants from the municipalities' own income and grants for equipment from the marshal. Another proposal is the granting of supervisory powers to the voivodeship as regards the activities of social inclusion centres, including the granting of appropriate supervisory instruments, such as a call for appropriate explanations and a call for the cessation or removal of irregularities found. The draft law amending the Social Employment Act and some other laws introduces the possibility for the Minister responsible for social security to create programmes for the development of social inclusion centres and clubs. Eight amendments were tabled at the Social Policy Committee meeting on 11 January. All amendments have been accepted. I will mainly discuss the substantive amendments, amendments that went beyond the scope of this law, as the Legislative Bureau pointed out. Nevertheless, the members of the committee did not object to these amendments, because they are very important from the point of view of the current situation in which our society, related to COVID, is located. There is also one amendment that applies to persons who receive supplementary benefits for persons unable to live on their own. Thus, in this Supplementary Provision Act, the amendment concerns the rule of increasing the amount of the monthly allowance threshold. We have introduced a provision that this threshold, this amount of entitlement to benefits, is valued annually on the same principles, as well as the valorization of pensions on 1 March each year. So we know that valorization is annual and that this rate of pension and pension valorization will also be raised. The two amendments I was talking about in connection with the Law on specific arrangements for preventing, combating and combating COVID-19, other communicable diseases and the crisis situations caused by them concern that those programmes which are implemented by the Regional Labour Offices, concerning the support of instruments, the support of employers in the labour market in connection with COVID, may also be applied after 2021, i.e. since 2022 these programmes have been extended from the Guaranteed Workers' Benefits Fund and the Labour Fund. These measures remain in the offices, these instruments will continue to be used. There is also a change, already more technical, in the Act on the Polish Tourist Bone. We know that it has been extended by six months for the use of these vouchers. Not all of them benefited, although I say it is a small group of people, children who have not yet used the gift. We want this to be a full use. We therefore postpone the deadline for the transmission of data collected in the electronic system until 31 December 2022. The other amendments were legislative in nature. I would like to thank all the members of the committee, the secretariat, the Minister very much. We didn't have any votes on this committee. All amendments and the submission of ministerial proposals in the draft law as well as all amendments were accepted without objection. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2022-01-12-sejm-46-1.u363	Dear Marshal! High Izbo! Minister! On behalf of the Law and Justice Club, I would like to present my position on this project. The reason for the necessary amendment of this law is the changing situation on the labour market, remaining without employment of the furthest from the labour market, often facing coupled life problems. As we learn from the justification for this law, it is the intention of the legislator to motivate people who are socially excluded from the concept of labour and social reintegration in order to develop adequate living attitudes, to perform social roles both in the workplace and in the place of residence, and to rebuild their ability to provide work on the labour market on their own. The social employment sector has been developing in Poland for 20 years now. Centres and clubs operate at the interface of two key areas of social policy, i.e. social assistance and labour market policy. It should be borne in mind that supporting social and professional reintegration in this form of social employment is not a compulsory task for local self-government units. The law regulates the possibility of reaching this form of education, shaping the local social situation, leaving the decision in this regard on the part of the actors who create them, i.e. on the part of local governments and NGOs. Together with the Social Employment Council, the solutions contained in the project facilitate inter alia. the functioning of social employment actors, the organisation of the reintegration process and the extension of certain powers of participants in centres. At the same time, the draft law provides solutions to improve the system of monitoring the activities of social employment actors, thus making the sector even more comprehensive. Changes in the social employment sector are due to different factors, but the situation on the labour market plays a key role. Although the rate of registered unemployment has steadily decreased over the last period, interestingly, the number of social employment actors and those benefiting from this support has increased in the same period. In this respect, the participation of people with disabilities and addicts to alcohol or drugs has increased systematically. Research and information provided by members of the Social Council confirm that other social problems are also common among the long-term unemployed. Changes in this area require measures to facilitate reintegration addressed both to participants and to lead actors. Further development of the social employment monitoring system is needed. The proposed changes have been developed, which should be highlighted, with the Social Employment Council. Now some of the most important issues that have been placed in this amendment. Firstly, it is a waiver of the obligation to meet the conditions of poverty for access to the instruments provided for in the project. This change is due to the fact that social exclusion is a fundamental issue determining whether to benefit from the support provided for in the Act. Poverty can be both the cause and the result of social exclusion and should therefore not have a decisive impact on access to the support law, which aims at social and professional inclusion. Secondly, and I think that this is a very important condition that is being examined, it is to allow the participation in clubs of persons who have a fixed right to an invalidity pension. So far they have not been able to participate, but after this amendment they will be able to do so. Thirdly, a very important change is an indication of the possibility of implementing reintegration services outside the location of the centres. The current regulation does not indicate where the services are to be provided to the participants of the centres. This situation has led to interpretation doubts and divergences in the practice of the operation of these entities. In some cases, it has been indicated that classes with participants can only take place at the place where the centres operate. This also led to a reduction in the development capacity of the centres and hindered access to the services of these entities for residents of remote areas from that indicated in the application for the status of the centre of operation. Such an interpretation also contributed to the creation of new centres in situations where it would be sufficient to extend the offer of existing entities, e.g. the possibility of organising integration activities outside the centre's place of operation, e.g. [[♪ The bell ♪]] in another town. The possibility of such organisation of the activities of the centres has been taken into account in the proposed amendments to the rules. Conducting part of the activities outside the centres is an opportunity to reach new groups and people at risk of social exclusion, which is particularly important for small towns. At the end of this speech, I would like to say that, in view of what I have put forward, as well as the discussion at the committee's meeting - substantive and, in principle, without opposition - as a representative of the Law and Justice Club, I recommend that the High House adopt this proposal. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]
ParlaMint-PL_2022-01-12-sejm-46-1.u364	Thank you very much, sir. Please, Mrs Marta Wcisło, the Civic Coalition.
ParlaMint-PL_2022-01-12-sejm-46-1.u365	Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! On behalf of the Civic Coalition, I have the honour to present my position on the Government's draft law amending the Social Employment Act and amending the Law on Sobriety Education and Against Alcoholism, print No. 1834. The Social Employment Act adopted in 2003 was intended to help those excluded from employment. In line with the rationale of the draft law, the aim was to make people affected or at risk of social exclusion independent by taking up vocational and social education in order to achieve their own efforts at economic independence, social prevention and the creation of institutional mechanisms for employment and the related re-adaptation and social inclusion for these people. The Act was adopted during a period of high and ever-increasing unemployment, which deepened the marginalisation of the most vulnerable groups. Today, 20 years later, we are in a slightly different situation when unemployment is no longer such a serious problem. There is no doubt, however, that there are still and there will be people excluded who need to be helped to become independent and return to the labour market. In this context, some of the changes contained in the proposed amendment should be considered to be right and could help achieve the objective of social inclusion centres and social inclusion clubs. It is acceptable to remove the conditions of poverty for access to the mechanisms provided for in the Act. There is no doubt that poverty is both the cause and effect of social exclusion and should not have a decisive impact on access to the support bill. Similarly, it should be assessed that participation in social inclusion clubs should be allowed for persons with disabilities who have a fixed right to an invalidity pension. However, there are doubts. If the amount of the integration benefit is increased to 120% unemployment benefit, instead of 100% so far , is a concept worthy of support, so there are doubts about the circumstances. First, the inclusion benefit is included in the income criterion. This is, in our opinion, an incomprehensible act. So far, the government has been guided by the concept of excluding cash benefits from the income criterion, e.g. 500+. The nature of the integration benefit also justifies such exclusion. Secondly, consideration should be given to introducing a mechanism to value the benefit in further work on the law, as well as the extent to which integration benefits are increased to a higher level. Thirdly, the bill assumes that the Social Employment Council will have at least 15 members. At present it is not more than 15. In our opinion, the proposed change is unfounded and there is no basis for it. The 15-person membership of the Social Employment Council is absolutely sufficient and there is no need expanding it. In addition, the draft law provides for an extension of the voivodeship's competence, which, upon request, will issue a decision to lose the status of the centre after the deadlines have expired in vain. This is a far-reaching rigour that does not take into account different situations. The Civic Coalition Club therefore proposes three amendments to the bill in this and above.

There are no social cooperatives of natural persons. In this respect, we add that both CIS and KIS can also create social cooperatives of individuals. The main changes to be made by this law relate primarily to an increase in social benefits of 100%. up to 120% so that it differs from unemployment benefits, which will allow more activation of excluded persons. The amendments make it possible to participate in social inclusion clubs for persons who have a fixed right to an invalidity pension. The new rules also allow the provision of reintegration services outside the place where social inclusion centres operate and extend the catalogue of entities entitled to create CISs and KISs. On the other hand, the reservation calls for a change enabling the voivod to take supervisory action towards social inclusion centres, as set out in Article. 1 points 5 and 6 of the draft law, which shows that it is the voivode who is to control and decide whether the entity in question will operate in the market for the next 5 years, where there is a clear need for its operation. The question therefore arises as to whether these centres should not operate indefinitely, which would ensure greater stability for those who use them and for those who are their founding bodies. This is due to the need to equip these institutions and the changing labour market. The catalogue of sources of funding has also changed, which has been opened up to new opportunities, such as resort schemes for the development of social employment actors. The amendment of the law also changed the weekly time of stay at the centre of social inclusion. To date, the standard has been mandatory 6 hours per day, but after the change, the participation will cover the whole week and will be at least 30 hours and not more than 40 hours per week. The number of days off from social inclusion centers has been increased to 12 and the days of incapacity due to illness have been increased to 21. In addition, the draft law amending the Social Employment Act and certain other laws contains a number of amendments of a more specific nature, which are right and necessary. It is important that these records have been consulted with local governments and many other institutions, as provided by the Minister yesterday at the meeting of the cultural committee. Therefore, on behalf of the parliamentary club of the Polish People's Party, I am making an appropriate amendment in this respect. Most of the solutions proposed in this law are right and necessary. Our club will vote in favour of this bill. And now I'm making an amendment to your marshal's hand. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2022-01-12-sejm-46-1.u401	Mrs. Marshal! Minister! High Izbo! The work of the Committee on Administration and Home Affairs consisted of two meetings of the committee and a nearly six-hour meeting of the subcommittee, at which we tried to develop the best model of this draft law, contained in print No 1833. The Government's draft legislation on passport documents concerns the construction of a new Register of Passport Documents carried out within the framework of the project, the development of the System of State Registers, i.e. the merger of the most important Polish registers, including the PESEL register, the Register of Personal Evidence, the Register of Civil Status and the Register of Contact Data, which will allow for easier and faster implementation of many cases of Polish citizens related to the wider administration and it will be possible to extend the scope of matters that citizens can carry out electronically regardless of the place where the case is initiated. On the one hand, I would like to emphasise the deep desire to work out the best possible solutions in this respect from the state administration, as well as from the ladies and gentlemen of Members. In this respect, it seems that it has been possible to improve or present new solutions under the existing document, for example, so that passports can be issued for 50% of the fees to firefighters of the voluntary fire department and to persons who were able to grant rescue services under the law that we have examined in this House on 17 December 2021, as well as to volunteers serving in mountain rescue units. This fee applies to banks that would have the chance to use the IT resources associated with issuing passports. The government side agreed to a log that says 0.5%. During the work on the final form of this law, we used many opinions, including the opinion issued by the Sejm Analysis Office, which gave an opinion at the request of the chairman of our committee, Mr Wiesław Szczepański, who asked whether replacing the currently used concept with one of the parents, with the return of the Sejm's mother and father, would not make it contrary to the concept used to date, its understanding in the light of other European Union rules which use them, among others. with our Act on Personal Evidence, the Law on Civil Status Records, the Practice so far and judicial case law. According to the opinion obtained, we have obtained such a sentence that the government draft legislation on passport documents replaces the currently used concept of "mother or father," and both the concept of "parent" and the concept of "mother or father" are currently used in the current normative acts. Therefore, the meaning of the terms in question seems to be similar, and they are most often used together with the concept of a guardian or guardian to indicate that a person who is a statutory representative of a minor may carry out certain activities or appropriate notifications to the authorities for his or her benefit. Dear Marshal! So much for my report. I am convinced that both the government and the ladies and gentlemen of Parliament will make comments in this reading, which will clarify the provisions of our report on our bill. I am convinced that the proposals contained in our committee's report meet, on the one hand, the letters, the proposals we received as Members and, on the other hand, they were common wisdom developed by Members and by the Government. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]
ParlaMint-PL_2022-01-12-sejm-46-1.u402	Thank you very much. ParlaMint-PL_2022-01-12-sejm-46-1.u403	Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! I have the honour to present the position of the Law and Justice Club regarding the government's draft passport bill, 1833 and 1898 prints. The present Act of 13 July 2006 on passport documents has been amended several times, bringing a number of important and expected social changes. However, all expectations stemming from changing socio-legal conditions have not been met. It is therefore necessary to make major changes to the system for issuing passport documents, which will improve and simplify the process of applying for and improving the security of the passport document. These objectives should be made using modern means of communication and new information technologies. It has therefore become necessary to re-draft legislation laying down rules for the implementation of passport cases. The proposed law provides for the construction of a new register of passport documents within the framework of the project, the development of a system of state registers, which is of strategic importance to the State. The project involves building new and modernising existing registers and setting standards for the incorporation of future state registers. The passport register will allow the launch of e-services for citizens, including the possibility of verifying the validity of the passport, verifying the correctness of the data collected in the passport register, ensuring direct and ongoing information to citizens about passport readiness, informing about changes in passport status. It will also be possible to immediately report the loss of a passport, increasing the safety of the person for whom the lost passport has been issued. As a result of the creation of a register of passport documents, traditional services will be modified, a paper version of the passport application will be removed. The application will be generated in the register of passport documents by the official and then signed by the applicant on the signature collection device. In addition, when receiving the passport document instead of the absolute requirement to confirm identity only on the basis of a valid identity document, it was also proposed that the identity of the person should be confirmed on the basis of a fingerprint by comparing the fingerprint of the recipient with the fingerprint recorded in the passport document. According to the proposed law, the proposed legislation sets out a catalogue of entities exempt from the obligation to pay a passport fee, a catalogue of entities entitled to pay a reduced passport fee, as well as statutory maximum limits for exemptions. The provisions of this act provide for a reduction to 12 years of age from which passports with a 10-year period of validity and signature will be issued. Persons under the age of 12 will receive a passport with a 5-year validity period. Another change in the period of validity of the passport document in relation to current legislation concerns the term of validity of the so-called. the second passport. In the current legal state, the second passport is valid for 2 years from the date of issue, and the proposed amendment implies extending the validity of the second passport to 3 years. The Act also provides for easier procedures for applying for a second passport. After the changes they will be issued by voivodeships and consuls, thereby taking over the competences of the Minister responsible for Home Affairs. In addition, the catalogue of persons who will be entitled to hold a temporary passport has been extended. It is about minors who, in connection with the continuation of education, the development of individual skills or the need to provide care for them, urgently need to travel abroad. The proposed law provides that the refusal to issue a passport document will be made by means of a decision issued by the passport authority, from which no appeal will be granted, and such action will have an impact on the summary shortening of the procedures for refusing to issue a passport document by a quicker decision by the administrative court as to whether or not it is justified, restricting the right of the citizen in this respect. The government bill deserves to be passed as soon as possible. Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! On behalf of the Parliamentary Club, I recommend that the law and justice continue to work on this bill without delay. Thank you. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2022-01-12-sejm-46-1.u459	Mr. Marshal! Minister! Ladies and gentlemen, ladies and gentlemen, On behalf of the Committee on Administration and Home Affairs, I have the honour to present in a concise manner a report on the Government's draft law amending the Passenger Name Record Processing Act in print No. 1891. Yesterday, the committee was investigating this very short document, a few articles, and I think that the focus of this amendment is elsewhere. It is related to the practical implementation and implementation of the 2016 European Parliament and Council Directive, which is reflected in the law adopted by the Polish Parliament in 2018. As you know, the Act applies to carriers operating passenger flights when the state border is exceeded or when an aircraft takes off or lands in our country. In order to safeguard the requirements of the Directive, the Polish legislator, i.e. parliament, adopted this full regulation in 2018, secured the performance by air carriers of the obligations related to the transfer of passenger flight data to the National Passenger Information Unit by the system of administrative penalties. The competent unit here is the Polish Border Guard. I refer in general to the justification of this project, following the need to repeat the arguments accompanying this important amendment. I think that, in particular, the aviation market is equally interested in it. As a reminder, this law imposed an obligation to implement these data within 6 months of the entry into force of the Act and to provide them by the air carrier to the Border Guard. When, after the entry into force of this law, these obligations were not fulfilled by some carriers, unfortunately, the provisions of that law, which required the Border Guard to charge penalties for failure to provide passenger data, were directly applicable. More than 109 000 have been identified since the entry into force of this law since May 2018. flights for which carriers have failed to comply with the obligation to transmit these data from the side, so as to say, purely administrative. The penalties imposed by the Chief Border Guard officer exceeded the amount of PLN 2197 million, and I would like to remind you that he has already spoken of this at the meeting and it has been repeated by the representative of the Minister of Home Affairs, the Deputy Minister of Administration and Home Affairs, that Member States are required to lay down rules on sanctions against carriers who do not provide such data in accordance with the rules and should be effective, proportionate and dissuasive. It is worth noting and I would also like to make it clear as rapporteur to you that there are currently no major problems with the implementation of this obligation on the part of air carriers. From the information, I think, very specific, very detailed, provided by the representative of the Chief Border Guard at yesterday's meeting of the committee, it appears that more than 150 air carriers who carry passengers in the territory of the Republic of Poland provide these data, so that today, you can say, practice is very good. Poland - together with France and the United Kingdom - according to the government's information is in the top three European countries, which have fully implemented in their legal order the one needed to combat crime, in particular terrorism, the Directive of the European Parliament and of the Council. This regulation, I am going through, you can say, on the issues relating to the proposals contained in the draft amendments, concerns the need to introduce in the Passenger Name Record Processing Act rules that would allow the imposition of financial penalties on air carriers during the process and, secondly, to accumulate administrative obligations in such a way that the authority of the Border Guard can carry out these procedures in a more efficient manner. Proposal to repeal this Article. The 70th Act aims primarily at the implementation of the Article. 189g of the Code of Administrative Procedure to the extent that limitation periods are determined on the basis of the provisions which will apply on the day of limitation of the infringement. The aim of the proposed amendments is also to allow for the transfer not only to the Chief Border Guard but also to the competent Head of Border Guard Branch of such a procedure, which, as I mentioned a moment earlier, may make the performance of tasks more flexible and may be important in a situation where infringements would be significant in the short term. Thus, the purpose of the procedure is to include more than one infringement in one case and to issue one decision in respect of a number of infringements. I think that this is primarily about the effective conduct of these actions, which concern a particular air carrier. As rapporteur for the committee, when submitting this report and asking the High Sejm to adopt the law, I would like to stress with satisfaction that yesterday there was a very, I think, detailed, necessary and mostly substantive discussion between, above all, the government party and the representatives of the aviation market. At least several air carriers operating in Poland were represented. Representatives of the International Air Carrier Association were also present. I think that the doubts about the transitional solution contained in the amended legislation have been clarified to a significant extent. I would also like to stress that the government side also in the discussion which concerned the matter related to the content of this law, i.e. the events which were or are related to the imposition of administrative penalties on carriers who, after the entry into force of this regulation in Poland, did not comply with the applicable rules. The Government Party has declared that it would ask the European Commission to take a position on a possible alternative solution that would move towards the most significant reduction of administrative penalties that were imposed in accordance with the law following the entry into the Polish legal system. I think that if there are questions later about whether there will be issues presented by the representatives of the clubs, I will perhaps allow myself to ask for a vote for a moment. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2022-01-12-sejm-46-1.u461	Mr. Marshal! High Seym! Minister! I was honored to give the position of Parliamentary Club Law and Justice to the government's draft law amending the Passenger Name Record Processing Act, Parliamentary Printing No. 1891. The mother law, which entered into force in May 2018, implemented in the Polish legal order the Directive of the European Parliament and of the Council of 27 April 2016 on the use of Passenger Name Record Data and forwarded to the National Passenger Information Unit for the prevention, detection, investigation and prosecution of terrorist offences and serious crime by air carriers under that law have been required to transmit to the National Passenger Information Unit set up by the Chief Border Guard Commandant all passengers before each flight between 48 and 24 hours. before the scheduled start of the flight and immediately after the completion of the ticket-baggage check-in and the boarding of passengers on the aircraft when they are no longer allowed to leave before the flight takes place and other passengers are not allowed to board. In the first period of application of the Act, more than 100 carriers failed to comply with their obligations. Today Poland is one of the leaders among the Member States of the European Union, both in terms of the number of carriers providing passenger data and the percentage of passengers whose data is transferred to the database. This means that as many as 152 air carriers are connected to the National Information System, which carry 99% passengers in Poland. At the same time, since 29 May 2018, i.e. from the date of entry into force of the mother's law, approximately 110 thousand were initially identified. flights for which air carriers have failed to comply with the obligation to transmit Passenger Name Record data. This means that they have committed the infringements referred to in Article. 64 laws, which are subject to an administrative penalty. As a result, the sum of penalties threatening carriers for the period from 29 May 2018 to 14 December 2021 is already at least PLN 2197 million. There is therefore a legitimate need to urgently introduce solutions that will establish a legal framework allowing for the longer-term imposition of financial penalties on air carriers and will reduce administrative obligations in this respect by cumulative proceedings and introducing the possibility of imposing administrative fines not only by the Chief Border Guard but also by the Head of Border Guard, which will make it more flexible in the Border Guard. The introduction of this option will be particularly important if there are a significant number of infringements in the short term. A number of infringements will be able to accumulate in a single procedure around 20 times the number of cases that will be brought to administrative courts, assuming that one procedure will be subject to an average of 20 flights for which infringements have occurred. Proposal to repeal the article. 70 The aim of the Act is to implement the limitation period resulting from the general principles, i.e. after the entry into force of the proposed Act. the provision of Article. 189g § 1 of the Code of Administrative Procedure, by which it was determined that the limitation periods would be determined on the basis of the provisions which would apply on the day of limitation of the infringement. The proposed amendment will safeguard the proper course of proceedings, especially in the case of particularly complex cases, in particular to allow the financial burden of imposing administrative fines on air carriers to be spread over time. Parliamentary Club Law and Justice supports the solutions contained in the government proposal, which are extremely important for the aviation industry, which is in a very difficult financial situation due to the continuing pandemic of SARS-CoV-2 virus, which has been adopted for this reason throughout the festival. It is worth recalling that the turnout of passengers at airports in Poland due to SARS-CoV-2 decreased by even more than 70% versus years before the pandemic. Therefore, Parliamentary Club Law and Justice does not amend the draft law annexed to the report of the Committee on Administration and Home Affairs in print No 1899 and recommends the adoption of this draft law to the High House. Thank you very much.

ParlaMint-PL_2022-01-12-sejm-46-1.u469	Thank you very much. Mr. Marshal! Minister! High Izbo! I have the honour on behalf of my club, the Polish Coalition Club - PSL, the Union of European Democrats, Marek Biernacki's Conservatives, to give a position on the draft law amending the Passenger Name Record Processing Act, which is included in Seym Print No. 1891. High Izbo! On 27 April 2016, Directive 2016/681 of the European Parliament and of the European Council on the use of passenger flight data entered into force, i.e. transfer of PNR data to prevent, detect, prosecute and prosecute terrorist offences and serious crime. Our Polish legislation has been adapted to this directive through the Act of 9 May 2018 on the Processing of Passenger Name Record Data. Under this law, carriers operating passenger flights in excess of Polish airspace, taking off or landing at our airports, were obliged to transfer PNR data to the National Passenger Information Unit. Failure to comply with this obligation resulted in the imposition of fines on carriers. This was done by the commandant of the Border Guard. Ready to proceed with the preparation of a programme for the transfer of PNR data within a time limit in accordance with Article. 76 Laws reported only 38 carriers. The rest were obliged to transmit data within 14 days of the date of entry into force of the Act I mentioned. There are already 152 carriers in the PNR system - this has been said in this Chamber - which generally meets the needs of around 99% passengers arriving in the territory of the Republic of Poland. Therefore, penalties were imposed for these deficiencies, the sum of which amounted to PLN 2197 million, but they are not administrative penalties, but are the result of an infringement of the Article. Ten bills. Art. The 14th Directive of the European Union states that Member States have been obliged to lay down sanctions, but not necessarily financial ones. The directive states that these sanctions are to be effective, proportionate, dissuasive. According to the information provided by the association of international carriers, penalties proportionate to the period of non-implementation of PLN 4 billion are likely to be imposed, of which 60 have already been imposed... High Izbo! We know very well that these penalties are being imposed in a time of pandemic, and this is the result of failing to comply with these obligations under the Act in the last three years. We know that, in general, this could bring down air carriers who are still in a pretty bad financial situation. It is also worth saying that before the pandemic each cruise generated about 3,000. PLN of profit, while in 2021 each trip generated losses of 17,5 thousand. zł. In 2020, only four regional ports generated profits. This deep crisis has further aggravated the increase in terminal charges by around 40% What came into force a few months ago. Around the airports, High Izbo, economic life is created. Companies and jobs are being created. The collapse of air carriers will result in the withdrawal of their offer from the Polish market, which will significantly reduce passenger traffic, as well as significantly affect the reduction of cargo transport, which could disrupt supply chains. It seems to us that, since other Member States do not impose additional penalties on carriers calculated in billions of zlotys for offences 3 years ago, we should approach the matter differently: we should send a request for interpretation to the European Commission and the European Parliament and aim to refrain from imposing these penalties, or to cancel them or to punish them symbolically, rather than extend the deadline for the next years. I'm talking about the limitation period. Yesterday, at the meeting of the Committee on Administration and Home Affairs, representatives of the aviation industry informed that they had not been consulted, but at the same time said that they were open to discussions and declared their cooperation with the Minister in this regard. To sum up, in the interests of passenger safety, as well as our country's security, we support, of course, the obligation to provide information, PNR data, but in a time of economic crisis, with the consequences of imposing penalties, we will not support this project. At the same time, we hope that the Ministry, the Minister, will decide on a dialogue with the aviation industry and will find a solution to this problem. Thank you kindly, Marshal.
