The draft law provides for amendments to these laws. The essence of the arrangements provided for in Article. 1 the draft law on payments under direct support schemes is to extend to 2022 the catalogue of available support instruments under the direct support scheme to complement the basic payment granted under the transitional national support. It should be stressed that this is support from the state budget. The granting of payments is also planned after 2022, but this will be regulated in the new rules that are being prepared for the implementation of the reform of the common agricultural policy for the period 2023 to 2027. As in 2013. - because this payment was already paid to farmers in 2013 under the previous perspective - the complementary basic payment will be allocated to the most important arable crops, including in particular cereals, oil crops, protein crops, and land lost. The detailed conditions for granting this payment, including the types of plants covered by the payment, will be laid down in a Regulation issued pursuant to Article. Article 34 (1) (a) and (b) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 1 and 2 of the amended act. On the other hand, the payment rate will be set in a regulation issued on the basis of the statutory authorisation added in Article. Article 20 (1) (b) and (c) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 2a. Restoration of the complementary basic payment due to its wide range - over one million farmers and almost nine million hectares - will increase the average level of support for the vast majority of farmers. This is a very important, important change, which will increase farmers' incomes. It will also reduce the gap between average direct support in Poland and the European Union. It is precisely this problem that there are significant differences between average income from farm work and average wages in the national economy. The entry into force of the amended law will somehow increase this revenue. Thus, according to the figures published by the CSO in 2020, e.g. The increase in average income from the family farm in 2020 per full-time person was almost 17% higher than the average income of the previous year, but this income is still less than the average income in the national economy. In 2020 it was almost 13% smaller. As already mentioned here, the deadline for receiving applications will be aligned with the deadline for receiving applications for all payments under the 2020 campaign, i.e. the period from 15 March to 15 May, of course taking into account an extension of 25 days, but this period of 25 days is already linked to the sanctions set out in the Act. The proposed date of entry into force is without prejudice to the principles of a democratic rule of law. The consultation on this law lasted from 19 November 2021 until 30 November 2021. For the implementation of the complementary basic payment in the 2022 campaign, 400 million PLN is planned, except that half of this amount, i.e. 200 million PLN, will be paid by the end of 2022 in the form of advances and in the form of payments from 1 December and the remaining part, 200 million PLN, in the framework of direct payments by the end of June 2023. [[The chairmanship of the proceedings includes the Marshal of the Sejm Elizabeth Witek]] We proceed with the examination of item 8 of the agenda: Report from the Commission on Energy, Climate and State Assets on the urgent draft law on specific arrangements for the protection of gas fuel customers in connection with the situation on the gas market (prints Nos 1902 and 1907). I ask Mr Wiesław Krajewski to present a report to the committee.
ParlaMint-PL_2022-01-12-sejm-46-1.u559	Dear Marshal! High Izbo! Dear Prime Minister, along with the Accompanying Ministers. It has been my honour to present a report on the work of the Committee on Energy, Climate and State Assets on the urgent draft of the Government's Law on Special Solutions for the Protection of Gas Fuels in view of the situation on the gas market, print No 1902. It is an extremely important and very necessary law that regulates certain current processes in order to protect Poland, Poles, households and many sensitive institutions that are exposed to these galloping gas prices, but it is also a project that tangiblely proves that the Polish government with Prime Minister Mateusz Morawiecki and the current Deputy Prime Minister Jack Sasin, who heads the Ministry of State Acts, responds to such dangers that threaten Poles, Polish families and the whole business that is based on gas, which is also needed for various types of production and technological processes. Today, i.e. On 12 January this year, the Commission for Energy, Climate and State Assets carried out its first reading of this draft law. First of all, I would like to stress that during the process of the proposed bill, all the members of the committee saw the seriousness of the current complex situation of rising gas prices, although it was not without enough emotional statements by the opposition Members trying to accuse the current government, the parliamentary majority, that they were carrying some Guilty for the current state of the gas crisis. And yet we know very well that the wine is absolutely on the side of the coalition of the Civic Platform and the ruling PSL in the years 2007 to 2015. [[Applause]] Already then appropriate steps had to be taken to safeguard the diversification of this energy source between Poland and Poles. An example, ladies and gentlemen, is the President of the Republic of Poland. Prof. Lech Kaczyński, who initiated the construction of a gas tank in Świnoujście. This is an example and it was worth continuing at that time. It is about energy security in the gas field in Poland. Mr Maciej Małecki, present at the committee meeting during the process of dealing with this law, specifically reminded the opposition of what could have been done in the area of energy security. Unfortunately, it can be said that little has been done at this time to become independent of our eastern neighbour, who has destabilised the whole of Europe in this area. After all, we all know that business is not the cause of the present difficult, crisis situation of gas. Due to the increased demand for gas in Europe and Asia and the simultaneous reduced supply of this fuel, the raw material from the main suppliers, mainly Russia, the prices of natural gas on global and European exchanges, including the Polish Freight Energy Exchange, are experiencing drastic, unprecedented growth. The extremely high level of gas prices in the European Union is largely influenced by the Russian Gazprom strategy of halting spot supplies to the European Union, abandoning the use of free capacity of existing transit routes, and pressing Gazprom warehouses in the European Union without rebuilding stocks before the winter season. The uniqueness of the current situation and the difficult time horizon for disturbances in the European Union's energy and gas markets mean that not only during the current heating season, but also during the spring and summer periods in 2022 gas prices will remain very high, which means that there is a high likelihood of a significant price increase again in the winter of 2022 and their continuation in the following years. In view of the above, it is not possible to predict the timing of the ongoing crisis and the extent and scale of the direct and indirect socio-economic impacts that have been felt by entrepreneurs, households, residents of multi-location buildings since the beginning of the heating season 2021/2022, as well as by other gas customers, in particular vulnerable customers, i.e. hospitals, schools, kindergartens, nursery schools, cultural institutions or other such facilities. Mandatory natural gas stocks are one of the key measures to combat the crisis in the natural gas market, in particular in the event of exceptional disruptions in the supply of this raw material or adverse weather conditions. In the past, mandatory stocks have protected the most vulnerable customers of natural gas as household customers from supply constraints, which confirmed the effectiveness of the measure. Enabling a state legal person, the Government's Strategic Reserve Agency, to take up part of the obligations relating to the maintenance of mandatory stocks of natural gas, guarantees an increase in the State's oversight of these stocks, thereby increasing the real capacity to take action in the event of an emergency. However, the limited supply of gas fuel on the market represents an important risk to increase the State's supervision of mandatory stocks. The changes envisaged in the project are aimed at fulfilling the State's obligation to provide access to the universal service covering the continuous and secure supply of gas to customers. At the same time, in order to minimise increases in gas fuel prices for the most vulnerable customers, and in order to increase the energy security of the State by ensuring the continuity of natural gas supplies to customers and ensuring an adequate level of liquidity for gas companies, there is a need to introduce specific shielding solutions to minimise the negative socio-economic impact of the sudden, rapid price increase of this raw material on the market. This bill will allow the launch of extraordinary instruments enhancing energy security of Poland, and in the short term supporting households, residents of multi-site buildings, as well as other vulnerable customers. During his work involving the first reading in the commission of the Deputy Minister of State Assets, Mr Maciej Małecki discussed in detail the draft law submitted, while appealing to Members of the opposition to support this law in a special way during this extraordinary period of some manipulation related to price increases, which is directed to the European area but also to Poland. The proposed solutions include the extension of the catalogue of operators covered by tariff protection, the introduction of a compensation mechanism for natural gas sellers, confirmation and emphasising in the provisions of the law the need for tariff protection of natural gas customers in multi-site buildings who have not concluded individual comprehensive contracts or contracts for the purchase of natural gas with energy undertakings. The project proposes extending tariff protection to the following entities: healthcare providers financed by public funds, social assistance organisations, accommodation and heating units, family support organisations and foster care systems, education system entities, higher education and science entities, crèches and children's clubs, churches and other religious associations, cultural entities, archival entities, voluntary firefighters. The extension of tariff protection is justified by the specific tasks carried out by these operators and the particularly severe effects of the rise in natural gas prices in terms of the feasibility of performing public service tasks. The project provides for the freezing of gas fuel prices at the price level specified in the ex officio seller's tariff - Article. 62c paragraph. 1 Act - Energy law - established on the basis of Article. 62f Act - Energy Law for the period from 1 January to 31 December 2022 At the same time, this price is of the nature of the maximum price, which means that the customers who have previously concluded a contract with a supplier providing a lower price are subject to this lower price until the end of the contract. Also, if price reductions were to occur during 2022 and traders started to offer prices lower than those set out in the ex officio tariffs, those prices would apply which would be reduced. At the same time, the proposed solutions exclude the possibility of increasing fuel prices and tariffs in 2022. In view of the application by the energy undertaking of the mechanism to spread over the time of the increase in the tariff price of natural gas for household customers, the draft law provides in this respect for compensation for the energy undertaking for each calendar month from the date on which the operator is entitled to apply the tariff calculated on the basis of the principles set out in Article. 62f of the above law. The total cost of compensation provided for by the Act shall not exceed PLN 10 billion. Compensation will be paid by the Difference Payment Fund Prices to which 40% will be paid in 2022 the funds obtained from the sale by auction of emission allowances. According to the records, this amount may not exceed PLN 6424 million. The COVID-19 Anti-Action Fund will be complemented. The draft law also provides for changes in the way in which mandatory stocks are implemented - the amendment of the Law of 16 February 2007 on oil stocks, petroleum products and natural gas and the rules for dealing with situations of threat to the fuel security of the State and disturbances on the oil market. According to the project, it will be possible to order mandatory natural gas stocks from entities to maintain these stocks also from the Government's Strategic Reserve Agency. According to Art. 20 draft law is proposed to enter into force on the day following the date of publication. The entry into force of the Act on the day following its publication is justified by the difficult situation of gas fuel customers affected by the gas price increase. The project provisions support households, residents of multi-site buildings and other vulnerable customers. I hope, ladies and gentlemen, that the Higher House, the Senate, in its democratic majority will also lean on the very necessary law of Poles and Poland as we do. I represent the applicants and present a position which coincides with our position as initiators of this amendment in the Act. The aim of this project is to eliminate local gas monopolies. Such monopolies exist in dozens of municipalities in the country, affecting tens of thousands of households. I come from the Poznań agglomeration. One such monopoly exists in our agglomeration, in the municipalities: Tarnowo Podgórne, Dopiewo, Buk, Kaźmierz, Opalenica, Grodzisk Wielkopolski, but such places are also located for example. in Puck in Lower Silesia, in the Wrocław agglomeration, in other places in Wielkopolska. The purpose of this law is to deregulate such local zones, closed zones in which companies, using the situation of the actual monopoly, sometimes unjustifiedly, and recently scandalously charged the very high gas costs of the inhabitants of these areas. At the end of September, e.g. in the Poznań agglomeration price increases were 170% which, of course, has affected many people who are deeply affected by this situation. A social protest committee was established. On the one hand, they outraged the prices. As I said, almost double the price increase by 170%. But there was a second element, one can say, dignity, because this gas infrastructure in the Poznań agglomeration was created as a result of the social work of the committees. As I said at the committee meeting, I remember this work because one of such committees in the municipality of Kaźmierz in Sokolniki Małe was led by my uncle Mieczysław Piotrowski. With great effort, the inhabitants of the Poznań agglomeration created this infrastructure to ensure the availability of gas in households. Unfortunately, as a result of the very unwise actions of the authorities of the municipality of Tarnowo Podgórne from the Civic Platform, this infrastructure was sold. It was a highly unwise act and the word 'unreasonably' is a word here, it seems to me, and so very delicate, all the more so given that the money from the sale was spent on the construction of the local swimming pool. One person protested during this sale ten years ago, she was Councilwoman Barbara Nowak of Law and Justice. I am pleased that, despite the sometimes slightly polemic tone of the energy committee meeting yesterday, the votes were positive. I am pleased that there is a consensus that this legal situation, these local monopolies, will weaken, deprive the rule that allows the expectations of the inhabitants to change the gas seller infinitely. I hope - and I would like to ask the High House - to continue this work as quickly as possible. Article 37 (1) (a) and (b) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 1 and Article 1 Article 40 (1) (a) and (b) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 On 11 January 2022, 1 of the Rules of Procedure of the Sejm sent the above draft law to the Committee on Administration and Home Affairs for first reading. The proposed amendments to the revision of the Strategic Reserves Act aim at extending the catalogue of created and maintained strategic reserves. The creation of the draft amendment is due to the fact that the current Strategic Reserves Act does not contain a sufficient level of regulation allowing rapid and efficient response in the field of police and military strategic reserves. The proposed amendment is not too extensive in its content - it contains only five articles - but it is extremely important due to the ongoing hybrid attack on our country and the organized nature of violations of Polish borders. The Committee on Administration and Home Affairs, after first reading and considering the above draft law at its meeting yesterday, asks the High Sejm to approve this very good and very necessary project in accordance with the Government's submission, and does not make any amendments.

In fact, there's been a breach. So far, there's been a rule that the gun is bought by the person who uses it. ParlaMint-PL_2022-01-12-sejm-46-1.u735	Mrs. Marshal! High Seym! 82 years ago, patients in the mental hospital were murdered in Chelm. How did this happen? In 1912 a solid building built by the Russian authorities was located on the outskirts of Chelm. In 1932, a nervous hospital was located there. In 1938 he counted 410 beds, served sick people from Lublin and Volyn Voivodeship. On January 6, 1940, a two - person commission came to this hospital from Lublin. They watched all the sick rooms, including the children's ward. On January 12, 1940, that is exactly 82 years ago, a squad of SS soldiers arrived from Lublin in two cars in the afternoon. There were 440 patients at the hospital that day. Things went pretty fast. Soldiers armed with machine guns immediately surrounded both hospital buildings. The officer ordered the medical, nursing and administrative staff to meet immediately in front of the main building and to leave the hospital within 10 minutes. They were allowed to take away the winter covers. So doctors, nurses, and nuns left the hospital. They were ordered to run fast and not look back. Ten nurses were ordered to remain. They were tasked with bringing 10 patients outside and handing them over to soldiers who were waiting with machine guns. Terrified sick people were pushed outside the entrance of the building, where bullets were about to reach them. They were falling for an increasing pile of dead bodies. Resistance Germans chased the halls of the building, caught throwing them out windows from the first and second floors. Then another building was taken. Some were dragged out of beds in shirts, barefoot, sometimes naked. The first victims were two priests, who were just after lunch on a walk in front of the building, a Greek Catholic priest Pawłowski from Vilnius, a former scientific worker who knew several languages, and Father Jan Makulec from Garwolin. When nearly 400 patients were killed and the blood turned white snow into red, children were finally brought out. Their catch was the hardest. Some of them smashed all over the building, hid under the beds and into the closets. They saw what was happening downstairs for hours. One of them was pulled out of the closet and thrown out of the window. Some were taken directly from their beds and carried behind their underwear to death. They were waving their hands and feet at it. The view was shocking. It was dark. The SS men, taking the small patients out, let them go a little further and shoot them from the vending machine. The kids were screaming and crying when they left the building. Their voices were mixed up with a bang of gunshots. 109 women, 18 children and 304 men were murdered. Nine people were saved from the massacre. The bodies of the murdered were lying up all night, some of them frozen to the ground next to a large pool of coagulated scarlet blood. They put five or six bodies on the vans and took them to a mass grave. This was used after a bomb dropped in 1939 on a dirt road along the sand excavation of a brick factory in Horodyszcz. Within a few days, hospital buildings were prepared for the SS barracks. Three weeks later, they were inhabited by those who killed their patients. The Germans completely destroyed the hospital records. Gestapo, the SS barracks, and then, in 1941, a military hospital were located in the buildings. When the Germans settled in the hospital, families used to bring their sick, not knowing anything about the tragedy of the hospital. The Germans dressed in aprons, pretended to be doctors and paramedics. When the families were barely outside the gate, the sick were shot in a dumpster. After the war, only 22 murdered adults and 17 children were able to recreate their names. The mass grave of the victims has been preserved until today. It is 150 m away from the place of execution, commemorated with a cross and a plaque. No one's ever been held accountable. Today is the 82nd anniversary of these events. May there never be a similar tragedy. Thank you. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2022-01-12-sejm-46-1.u748	Declaration by the Members on the deteriorating situation of public transport. Dear Marshal! High Izbo! The COVID-19 Pandemic changed irreversibly our reality - professional, private or economic, with its consequences must be faced by everyone today. The Pandemic has also not missed public transport carriers. Passengers are getting less and less, not only because of fear of traveling by public transport, but also because of the popularity of remote work, multi-month closing of schools, cinemas, restaurants or theatres. Today, there are high energy prices and tax changes that will affect the finances of local governments. Some local authorities have already decided to raise ticket prices, of which passengers are dissatisfied. Public transport today benefits around 20%. less passengers than before the pandemic, and it may take another two years to return to its original state. However, these forecasts depend on many factors. The first is the further development of the fourth wave of pandemics and the spread of the new option of Coronavirus, while the second is rising electricity prices. We look at both of these factors with concern. No one can guarantee today that within two years, we will overcome the effects of the pandemic. Ticket price increases seem inevitable, and in many Polish cities they have become a fact. Self-governments are concerned not only about the lower income from PIT, but also about the lack of support from EU money, which is halted by the European Commission's negotiations with the government. The worsening situation of carriers operating in the public transport sector is worrying. The lack of support causes more entrepreneurs to withdraw from the market or change the industry to a more stable one. We must not allow a situation where the demand for public transport is greater than the possibility to provide these services.
ParlaMint-PL_2022-01-12-sejm-46-1.u749	Parliamentary statement on the newly opened branch of the Lower Silesian Railways in Jaworzyna Śląska. Dear Marshal! High Izbo! Rail transport in Poland plays a significant role in moving both cargo and passengers. Rail meets the needs of its users: it enables access to work, schools, universities and also allows for recreational travel. The rail network is growing from year to year and passengers are able to use an increasing number of connections. Equally optimistic is the prospect of the development of railway networks in Poland, which assumes over PLN 250 billion worth of investments in the next 20 years. The investment plan includes both the modernisation of existing lines and the construction of new ones. Both PKP PLK and railway carriers belonging to regional governments are constantly developing. This is the case with the Dolnośląskie Railways, owned by the local government of the Dolnośląskie Voivodeship. The Management Board of the company informed about the creation of a new branch in Jaworzyna Śląska, where one of the most important railway hubs in Lower Silesia is located. Jaworzyna Śląska will be the fifth location where the Dolnośląskie Railway teams will be stationed. So far there have been four facilities, located in Jelenia Góra, Wrocław, Kłodzk and Legnica, where the main seat of the carrier is located. The change is dictated by staff needs related to the purchase of new rolling stock or the launching of new railway connections. The choice of Silesian Jaworsina remains not accidental. The location and good communication contributed to the creation of a new branch of the Dolnośląskie Railways. In Jaworzyna Śląska thanks to the intersection of several railway lines it will be possible to optimize traffic and emergency response capabilities. This is excellent news for passengers who will be able to optimize their journey, but also for the carrier, which thanks to the growing number of travelers can develop. The newly opened branch in Jaworzyn Śląska will certainly contribute to the development of the Dolnośląskie voivodship, and tourists visiting our region will be able to benefit from more convenient railway connections for themselves.
